        Idea:
Refactor the video editor timeline drag system (useClipDrag.ts and related) to be elegant and well-engineered. The current system has these problems we've identified:

1. **Two competing pointer event systems**: useClipDrag bolts a capture-phase document listener on top of the timeline library's own pointer system, coordinated via a fragile string-mutex gestureOwner ref. If either side fails to release ownership, the other is permanently locked out.

2. **Pointer capture + React re-renders = race condition**: setPointerCapture pins events to a DOM element, but React can replace that element during re-render, silently releasing capture and potentially orphaning the session.

3. **The button element fights you**: Native button focus, activation, click synthesis, and drag behavior all interfere with the custom pointer system. stopPropagation() on pointerdown creates asymmetry between capture and bubble phases.

4. **Session lifecycle is optimistic**: Every pointerdown creates a mutable DragSession with 20+ fields. Cleanup relies on pointerup/pointercancel always firing with matching pointerId. Leaked sessions leave listeners on window, pendingOps stuck > 0, and gestureOwner stuck.

5. **600-line effect closure with 15 ref-of-ref indirections**: The entire drag system lives in one useEffect that never re-runs. Every changing value is smuggled through refs (selectedClipIdsRefRef, pendingOpsRefRef, etc.). Nothing is statically traceable.

6. **DragSession is a grab-bag**: Mixes pointer tracking, DOM references, input classification, gesture state, and event handlers in one mutated object.

The well-engineered version should have:
- **Single event coordinator** instead of two competing systems
- **AbortController pattern** for atomic listener cleanup (no leaked sessions)
- **No pointer capture** — just window listeners + coordinator state machine
- **div role=button instead of button** — same a11y, no native behaviors to fight
- **Explicit state machine**: idle → pending → dragging → idle, with each transition having exactly one set of side effects
- **Derived values, not accumulated mutations**: ghost position and snap as pure functions of (dragState, pointerPosition, timelineData)
- **No pendingOps counter** — just read dragState !== idle

Key files:
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.ts (main target, ~760 lines)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/ClipAction.tsx (button → div role=button, remove stopPropagation)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useMarqueeSelect.ts (the other pointer system that competes)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx (wires everything together)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.test.tsx (tests)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/mobile-interaction-model.ts (gesture owner types)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/interaction-state.ts (interaction state ref)

The goal is to make this system simple, correct, and debuggable while preserving all existing functionality (single clip drag, multi-clip drag, cross-track drag, shot group drag, marquee select, click-to-select, modifier key selection, snap-to-edge, auto-scroll during drag).

The current code at line 744 uses capture phase (`true`). The refactored code switches to bubble phase, which works because Steps 1-3 remove all `stopPropagation` on pointerdown from ClipAction and the shot-group label. The `wrapper.contains()` containment check at line 330-331 scopes events correctly.\n\n**SD-005 \u2014 Keyboard activation on div[role=button]**: Don't invent new behavior. The current native `<button>` provides implicit Enter/Space activation that fires click. Since there's no root onClick handler on ClipAction, and selection is driven by useClipDrag pointer events, simply adding `tabIndex={0}` for focusability is sufficient. Don't add an `onKeyDown` for Enter/Space unless you can map it to `onSelect` \u2014 and even then, `onSelect` is a menu helper, not click-to-select. Keep it simple.\n\n**Overflow trigger**: The overflow trigger at ClipAction.tsx:475-499 is already a `<div role=\"button\">` with `onPointerDown stopPropagation`. The plan says: add `data-no-clip-drag` attribute to it AND remove its `onPointerDown stopPropagation`. But be careful \u2014 the overflow trigger's stopPropagation also prevents the *outer* button/div's pointerdown from firing. Since we're converting the outer element from `<button>` to `<div>`, there's no native button activation to worry about, but the event *will* bubble to document where useClipDrag's listener will see it. That's why `data-no-clip-drag` is needed.\n\n**Step 7 is the big one**: The main useEffect rewrite (lines 176-753) is ~580 lines. The refactored version should use the state machine pattern with AbortController. Key things to get right:\n1. `endSession` must be callable from any state and idempotent\n2. `pendingOpsRef.current += 1` happens in handlePointerDown (on entering pending), `pendingOpsRef.current -= 1` happens in endSession\n3. `interactionState.drag = true` and `setGestureOwner('clip')` happen on threshold cross (pending\u2192dragging), not on pointerdown\n4. `dragSessionRef.current` must be set to a backward-compatible object on entering pending (not just dragging), because TimelineCanvas checks truthiness to hide shot-group overlays during any active drag session\n5. The `deferDeactivate` pattern (rAF for crossTrackActive reset) must be preserved for cross-track drops\n\n**Ref consolidation**: Replace the 15 individual ref pairs (lines 147-174) with a single `latestRef = useRef({...})` pattern. Update on every render: `latestRef.current = { coordinator, moveClipToRow, ... }`. In handlers, read `latestRef.current.coordinator` instead of `coordinatorRef.current`.\n\n**CSS selectors**: The plan assumes styling uses `.clip-action` class selectors, not `button.clip-action`. - [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant. - [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies — even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.         Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace

        Idea:
Refactor the video editor timeline drag system (useClipDrag.ts and related) to be elegant and well-engineered. The current system has these problems we've identified:

1. **Two competing pointer event systems**: useClipDrag bolts a capture-phase document listener on top of the timeline library's own pointer system, coordinated via a fragile string-mutex gestureOwner ref. If either side fails to release ownership, the other is permanently locked out.

2. **Pointer capture + React re-renders = race condition**: setPointerCapture pins events to a DOM element, but React can replace that element during re-render, silently releasing capture and potentially orphaning the session.

3. **The button element fights you**: Native button focus, activation, click synthesis, and drag behavior all interfere with the custom pointer system. stopPropagation() on pointerdown creates asymmetry between capture and bubble phases.

4. **Session lifecycle is optimistic**: Every pointerdown creates a mutable DragSession with 20+ fields. Cleanup relies on pointerup/pointercancel always firing with matching pointerId. Leaked sessions leave listeners on window, pendingOps stuck > 0, and gestureOwner stuck.

5. **600-line effect closure with 15 ref-of-ref indirections**: The entire drag system lives in one useEffect that never re-runs. Every changing value is smuggled through refs (selectedClipIdsRefRef, pendingOpsRefRef, etc.). Nothing is statically traceable.

6. **DragSession is a grab-bag**: Mixes pointer tracking, DOM references, input classification, gesture state, and event handlers in one mutated object.

The well-engineered version should have:
- **Single event coordinator** instead of two competing systems
- **AbortController pattern** for atomic listener cleanup (no leaked sessions)
- **No pointer capture** — just window listeners + coordinator state machine
- **div role=button instead of button** — same a11y, no native behaviors to fight
- **Explicit state machine**: idle → pending → dragging → idle, with each transition having exactly one set of side effects
- **Derived values, not accumulated mutations**: ghost position and snap as pure functions of (dragState, pointerPosition, timelineData)
- **No pendingOps counter** — just read dragState !== idle

Key files:
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.ts (main target, ~760 lines)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/ClipAction.tsx (button → div role=button, remove stopPropagation)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useMarqueeSelect.ts (the other pointer system that competes)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx (wires everything together)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.test.tsx (tests)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/mobile-interaction-model.ts (gesture owner types)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/interaction-state.ts (interaction state ref)

The goal is to make this system simple, correct, and debuggable while preserving all existing functionality (single clip drag, multi-clip drag, cross-track drag, shot group drag, marquee select, click-to-select, modifier key selection, snap-to-edge, auto-scroll during drag).

  "meta_commentary": "## Critical Execution Guidance\n\n**Test baseline**: 15 tests (not 17 as stated in the plan) pass in `useClipDrag.test.tsx`. The correct test command is:\n```\ncd reigh-app && npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.test.tsx\n```\n\n**SD-004 \u2014 Document listener in BUBBLE phase**: The gate settled that useClipDrag must use `document.addEventListener('pointerdown', handlePointerDown)` in **bubble phase** (no `true` third arg), NOT on the wrapper element. The current code at line 744 uses capture phase (`true`). The refactored code switches to bubble phase, which works because Steps 1-3 remove all `stopPropagation` on pointerdown from ClipAction and the shot-group label. The `wrapper.contains()` containment check at line 330-331 scopes events correctly.\n\n**SD-005 \u2014 Keyboard activation on div[role=button]**: Don't invent new behavior. The current native `<button>` provides implicit Enter/Space activation that fires click. Since there's no root onClick handler on ClipAction, and selection is driven by useClipDrag pointer events, simply adding `tabIndex={0}` for focusability is sufficient. Don't add an `onKeyDown` for Enter/Space unless you can map it to `onSelect` \u2014 and even then, `onSelect` is a menu helper, not click-to-select. Keep it simple.\n\n**Overflow trigger**: The overflow trigger at ClipAction.tsx:475-499 is already a `<div role=\"button\">` with `onPointerDown stopPropagation`. The plan says: add `data-no-clip-drag` attribute to it AND remove its `onPointerDown stopPropagation`. But be careful \u2014 the overflow trigger's stopPropagation also prevents the *outer* button/div's pointerdown from firing. Since we're converting the outer element from `<button>` to `<div>`, there's no native button activation to worry about, but the event *will* bubble to document where useClipDrag's listener will see it. That's why `data-no-clip-drag` is needed.\n\n**Step 7 is the big one**: The main useEffect rewrite (lines 176-753) is ~580 lines. The refactored version should use the state machine pattern with AbortController. Key things to get right:\n1. `endSession` must be callable from any state and idempotent\n2. `pendingOpsRef.current += 1` happens in handlePointerDown (on entering pending), `pendingOpsRef.current -= 1` happens in endSession\n3. `interactionState.drag = true` and `setGestureOwner('clip')` happen on threshold cross (pending\u2192dragging), not on pointerdown\n4. `dragSessionRef.current` must be set to a backward-compatible object on entering pending (not just dragging), because TimelineCanvas checks truthiness to hide shot-group overlays during any active drag session\n5. The `deferDeactivate` pattern (rAF for crossTrackActive reset) must be preserved for cross-track drops\n\n**Ref consolidation**: Replace the 15 individual ref pairs (lines 147-174) with a single `latestRef = useRef({...})` pattern. Update on every render: `latestRef.current = { coordinator, moveClipToRow, ... }`. In handlers, read `latestRef.current.coordinator` instead of `coordinatorRef.current`.\n\n**CSS selectors**: The plan assumes styling uses `.clip-action` class selectors, not `button.clip-action`. Verify no CSS in the project uses `button.clip-action` before changing the element type.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies — even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable: are the success criteria well-prioritized and verifiable?: the attached success criteria are stale and do not match the current plan body. they still require 'no `usestate` for `activepairdata`', '`setactivepairdata` fully removed', 'sync effect deleted', and '`onpairclick` simplified to `(pairindex: number) => void`', which are the opposite of the current targeted-sync plan. as presented, the criteria are not usable for review or execution. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace

        Idea:
Refactor the video editor timeline drag system (useClipDrag.ts and related) to be elegant and well-engineered. The current system has these problems we've identified:

1. **Two competing pointer event systems**: useClipDrag bolts a capture-phase document listener on top of the timeline library's own pointer system, coordinated via a fragile string-mutex gestureOwner ref. If either side fails to release ownership, the other is permanently locked out.

2. **Pointer capture + React re-renders = race condition**: setPointerCapture pins events to a DOM element, but React can replace that element during re-render, silently releasing capture and potentially orphaning the session.

3. **The button element fights you**: Native button focus, activation, click synthesis, and drag behavior all interfere with the custom pointer system. stopPropagation() on pointerdown creates asymmetry between capture and bubble phases.

4. **Session lifecycle is optimistic**: Every pointerdown creates a mutable DragSession with 20+ fields. Cleanup relies on pointerup/pointercancel always firing with matching pointerId. Leaked sessions leave listeners on window, pendingOps stuck > 0, and gestureOwner stuck.

5. **600-line effect closure with 15 ref-of-ref indirections**: The entire drag system lives in one useEffect that never re-runs. Every changing value is smuggled through refs (selectedClipIdsRefRef, pendingOpsRefRef, etc.). Nothing is statically traceable.

6. **DragSession is a grab-bag**: Mixes pointer tracking, DOM references, input classification, gesture state, and event handlers in one mutated object.

The well-engineered version should have:
- **Single event coordinator** instead of two competing systems
- **AbortController pattern** for atomic listener cleanup (no leaked sessions)
- **No pointer capture** — just window listeners + coordinator state machine
- **div role=button instead of button** — same a11y, no native behaviors to fight
- **Explicit state machine**: idle → pending → dragging → idle, with each transition having exactly one set of side effects
- **Derived values, not accumulated mutations**: ghost position and snap as pure functions of (dragState, pointerPosition, timelineData)
- **No pendingOps counter** — just read dragState !== idle

Key files:
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.ts (main target, ~760 lines)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/ClipAction.tsx (button → div role=button, remove stopPropagation)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useMarqueeSelect.ts (the other pointer system that competes)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx (wires everything together)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.test.tsx (tests)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/mobile-interaction-model.ts (gesture owner types)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/interaction-state.ts (interaction state ref)

The goal is to make this system simple, correct, and debuggable while preserving all existing functionality (single clip drag, multi-clip drag, cross-track drag, shot group drag, marquee select, click-to-select, modifier key selection, snap-to-edge, auto-scroll during drag).

The correct test command is:\n```\ncd reigh-app && npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.test.tsx\n```\n\n**SD-004 \u2014 Document listener in BUBBLE phase**: The gate settled that useClipDrag must use `document.addEventListener('pointerdown', handlePointerDown)` in **bubble phase** (no `true` third arg), NOT on the wrapper element. The current code at line 744 uses capture phase (`true`). The refactored code switches to bubble phase, which works because Steps 1-3 remove all `stopPropagation` on pointerdown from ClipAction and the shot-group label. The `wrapper.contains()` containment check at line 330-331 scopes events correctly.\n\n**SD-005 \u2014 Keyboard activation on div[role=button]**: Don't invent new behavior. The current native `<button>` provides implicit Enter/Space activation that fires click. Since there's no root onClick handler on ClipAction, and selection is driven by useClipDrag pointer events, simply adding `tabIndex={0}` for focusability is sufficient. Don't add an `onKeyDown` for Enter/Space unless you can map it to `onSelect` \u2014 and even then, `onSelect` is a menu helper, not click-to-select. Keep it simple.\n\n**Overflow trigger**: The overflow trigger at ClipAction.tsx:475-499 is already a `<div role=\"button\">` with `onPointerDown stopPropagation`. The plan says: add `data-no-clip-drag` attribute to it AND remove its `onPointerDown stopPropagation`. But be careful \u2014 the overflow trigger's stopPropagation also prevents the *outer* button/div's pointerdown from firing. Since we're converting the outer element from `<button>` to `<div>`, there's no native button activation to worry about, but the event *will* bubble to document where useClipDrag's listener will see it. That's why `data-no-clip-drag` is needed.\n\n**Step 7 is the big one**: The main useEffect rewrite (lines 176-753) is ~580 lines. The refactored version should use the state machine pattern with AbortController. Key things to get right:\n1. `endSession` must be callable from any state and idempotent\n2. `pendingOpsRef.current += 1` happens in handlePointerDown (on entering pending), `pendingOpsRef.current -= 1` happens in endSession\n3. `interactionState.drag = true` and `setGestureOwner('clip')` happen on threshold cross (pending\u2192dragging), not on pointerdown\n4. `dragSessionRef.current` must be set to a backward-compatible object on entering pending (not just dragging), because TimelineCanvas checks truthiness to hide shot-group overlays during any active drag session\n5. The `deferDeactivate` pattern (rAF for crossTrackActive reset) must be preserved for cross-track drops\n\n**Ref consolidation**: Replace the 15 individual ref pairs (lines 147-174) with a single `latestRef = useRef({...})` pattern. Update on every render: `latestRef.current = { coordinator, moveClipToRow, ... }`. In handlers, read `latestRef.current.coordinator` instead of `coordinatorRef.current`.\n\n**CSS selectors**: The plan assumes styling uses `.clip-action` class selectors, not `button.clip-action`. Verify no CSS in the project uses `button.clip-action` before changing the element type.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies — even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable: are the success criteria well-prioritized and verifiable?: the attached success criteria are stale and do not match the current plan body. they still require 'no `usestate` for `activepairdata`', '`setactivepairdata` fully removed', 'sync effect deleted', and '`onpairclick` simplified to `(pairindex: number) => void`', which are the opposite of the current targeted-sync plan. as presented, the criteria are not usable for review or execution. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace

        Idea:
Refactor the video editor timeline drag system (useClipDrag.ts and related) to be elegant and well-engineered. The current system has these problems we've identified:

1. **Two competing pointer event systems**: useClipDrag bolts a capture-phase document listener on top of the timeline library's own pointer system, coordinated via a fragile string-mutex gestureOwner ref. If either side fails to release ownership, the other is permanently locked out.

2. **Pointer capture + React re-renders = race condition**: setPointerCapture pins events to a DOM element, but React can replace that element during re-render, silently releasing capture and potentially orphaning the session.

3. **The button element fights you**: Native button focus, activation, click synthesis, and drag behavior all interfere with the custom pointer system. stopPropagation() on pointerdown creates asymmetry between capture and bubble phases.

4. **Session lifecycle is optimistic**: Every pointerdown creates a mutable DragSession with 20+ fields. Cleanup relies on pointerup/pointercancel always firing with matching pointerId. Leaked sessions leave listeners on window, pendingOps stuck > 0, and gestureOwner stuck.

5. **600-line effect closure with 15 ref-of-ref indirections**: The entire drag system lives in one useEffect that never re-runs. Every changing value is smuggled through refs (selectedClipIdsRefRef, pendingOpsRefRef, etc.). Nothing is statically traceable.

6. **DragSession is a grab-bag**: Mixes pointer tracking, DOM references, input classification, gesture state, and event handlers in one mutated object.

The well-engineered version should have:
- **Single event coordinator** instead of two competing systems
- **AbortController pattern** for atomic listener cleanup (no leaked sessions)
- **No pointer capture** — just window listeners + coordinator state machine
- **div role=button instead of button** — same a11y, no native behaviors to fight
- **Explicit state machine**: idle → pending → dragging → idle, with each transition having exactly one set of side effects
- **Derived values, not accumulated mutations**: ghost position and snap as pure functions of (dragState, pointerPosition, timelineData)
- **No pendingOps counter** — just read dragState !== idle

Key files:
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.ts (main target, ~760 lines)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/ClipAction.tsx (button → div role=button, remove stopPropagation)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useMarqueeSelect.ts (the other pointer system that competes)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx (wires everything together)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.test.tsx (tests)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/mobile-interaction-model.ts (gesture owner types)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/interaction-state.ts (interaction state ref)

The goal is to make this system simple, correct, and debuggable while preserving all existing functionality (single clip drag, multi-clip drag, cross-track drag, shot group drag, marquee select, click-to-select, modifier key selection, snap-to-edge, auto-scroll during drag).

        Batch framing:
        - Execute batch 4 of 6.
        - Actionable task IDs for this batch: ['T6']
        - Already completed task IDs available as dependency context: ['T1', 'T2', 'T3', 'T4', 'T5']

  {
    "id": "T6",
    "description": "Phase 2, Steps 7-8: Rewrite the main useEffect with the state machine and AbortController pattern. Key changes: (1) Replace `dragSessionRef` internal usage with `stateRef = useRef<DragMachineState>({ phase: 'idle' })`. (2) Implement `endSession` with `controller.abort()` for atomic cleanup \u2014 no manual removeEventListener. (3) Remove all setPointerCapture/releasePointerCapture. (4) Switch document listener from capture phase to BUBBLE phase (remove `true` from addEventListener). (5) handlePointerMove: check threshold in pending phase, transition to dragging on cross, set interactionState.drag and gestureOwner only on transition. (6) handlePointerUp: selection logic in pending phase, commit logic in dragging phase. (7) Maintain backward-compatible `dragSessionRef.current` \u2014 set to a subset object on entering pending, null on entering idle. Must include clipId, draggedClipIds, groupDragEntry for test compat and truthiness for TimelineCanvas/useMarqueeSelect. (8) Preserve deferDeactivate rAF for crossTrackActive reset on cross-track drops. File: `useClipDrag.ts`.",

  "meta_commentary": "## Critical Execution Guidance\n\n**Test baseline**: 15 tests (not 17 as stated in the plan) pass in `useClipDrag.test.tsx`. The correct test command is:\n```\ncd reigh-app && npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.test.tsx\n```\n\n**SD-004 \u2014 Document listener in BUBBLE phase**: The gate settled that useClipDrag must use `document.addEventListener('pointerdown', handlePointerDown)` in **bubble phase** (no `true` third arg), NOT on the wrapper element. The current code at line 744 uses capture phase (`true`). The refactored code switches to bubble phase, which works because Steps 1-3 remove all `stopPropagation` on pointerdown from ClipAction and the shot-group label. The `wrapper.contains()` containment check at line 330-331 scopes events correctly.\n\n**SD-005 \u2014 Keyboard activation on div[role=button]**: Don't invent new behavior. The current native `<button>` provides implicit Enter/Space activation that fires click. Since there's no root onClick handler on ClipAction, and selection is driven by useClipDrag pointer events, simply adding `tabIndex={0}` for focusability is sufficient. Don't add an `onKeyDown` for Enter/Space unless you can map it to `onSelect` \u2014 and even then, `onSelect` is a menu helper, not click-to-select. Keep it simple.\n\n**Overflow trigger**: The overflow trigger at ClipAction.tsx:475-499 is already a `<div role=\"button\">` with `onPointerDown stopPropagation`. The plan says: add `data-no-clip-drag` attribute to it AND remove its `onPointerDown stopPropagation`. But be careful \u2014 the overflow trigger's stopPropagation also prevents the *outer* button/div's pointerdown from firing. Since we're converting the outer element from `<button>` to `<div>`, there's no native button activation to worry about, but the event *will* bubble to document where useClipDrag's listener will see it. That's why `data-no-clip-drag` is needed.\n\n**Step 7 is the big one**: The main useEffect rewrite (lines 176-753) is ~580 lines. The refactored version should use the state machine pattern with AbortController. Key things to get right:\n1. `endSession` must be callable from any state and idempotent\n2. `pendingOpsRef.current += 1` happens in handlePointerDown (on entering pending), `pendingOpsRef.current -= 1` happens in endSession\n3. `interactionState.drag = true` and `setGestureOwner('clip')` happen on threshold cross (pending\u2192dragging), not on pointerdown\n4. `dragSessionRef.current` must be set to a backward-compatible object on entering pending (not just dragging), because TimelineCanvas checks truthiness to hide shot-group overlays during any active drag session\n5. The `deferDeactivate` pattern (rAF for crossTrackActive reset) must be preserved for cross-track drops\n\n**Ref consolidation**: Replace the 15 individual ref pairs (lines 147-174) with a single `latestRef = useRef({...})` pattern. Update on every render: `latestRef.current = { coordinator, moveClipToRow, ... }`. In handlers, read `latestRef.current.coordinator` instead of `coordinatorRef.current`.\n\n**CSS selectors**: The plan assumes styling uses `.clip-action` class selectors, not `button.clip-action`. Verify no CSS in the project uses `button.clip-action` before changing the element type.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies — even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable: are the success criteria well-prioritized and verifiable?: the attached success criteria are stale and do not match the current plan body. they still require 'no `usestate` for `activepairdata`', '`setactivepairdata` fully removed', 'sync effect deleted', and '`onpairclick` simplified to `(pairindex: number) => void`', which are the opposite of the current targeted-sync plan. as presented, the criteria are not usable for review or execution. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace

        Idea:
Refactor the video editor timeline drag system (useClipDrag.ts and related) to be elegant and well-engineered. The current system has these problems we've identified:

1. **Two competing pointer event systems**: useClipDrag bolts a capture-phase document listener on top of the timeline library's own pointer system, coordinated via a fragile string-mutex gestureOwner ref. If either side fails to release ownership, the other is permanently locked out.

2. **Pointer capture + React re-renders = race condition**: setPointerCapture pins events to a DOM element, but React can replace that element during re-render, silently releasing capture and potentially orphaning the session.

3. **The button element fights you**: Native button focus, activation, click synthesis, and drag behavior all interfere with the custom pointer system. stopPropagation() on pointerdown creates asymmetry between capture and bubble phases.

4. **Session lifecycle is optimistic**: Every pointerdown creates a mutable DragSession with 20+ fields. Cleanup relies on pointerup/pointercancel always firing with matching pointerId. Leaked sessions leave listeners on window, pendingOps stuck > 0, and gestureOwner stuck.

5. **600-line effect closure with 15 ref-of-ref indirections**: The entire drag system lives in one useEffect that never re-runs. Every changing value is smuggled through refs (selectedClipIdsRefRef, pendingOpsRefRef, etc.). Nothing is statically traceable.

6. **DragSession is a grab-bag**: Mixes pointer tracking, DOM references, input classification, gesture state, and event handlers in one mutated object.

The well-engineered version should have:
- **Single event coordinator** instead of two competing systems
- **AbortController pattern** for atomic listener cleanup (no leaked sessions)
- **No pointer capture** — just window listeners + coordinator state machine
- **div role=button instead of button** — same a11y, no native behaviors to fight
- **Explicit state machine**: idle → pending → dragging → idle, with each transition having exactly one set of side effects
- **Derived values, not accumulated mutations**: ghost position and snap as pure functions of (dragState, pointerPosition, timelineData)
- **No pendingOps counter** — just read dragState !== idle

Key files:
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.ts (main target, ~760 lines)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/ClipAction.tsx (button → div role=button, remove stopPropagation)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useMarqueeSelect.ts (the other pointer system that competes)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx (wires everything together)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.test.tsx (tests)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/mobile-interaction-model.ts (gesture owner types)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/interaction-state.ts (interaction state ref)

The goal is to make this system simple, correct, and debuggable while preserving all existing functionality (single clip drag, multi-clip drag, cross-track drag, shot group drag, marquee select, click-to-select, modifier key selection, snap-to-edge, auto-scroll during drag).

  "meta_commentary": "## Critical Execution Guidance\n\n**Test baseline**: 15 tests (not 17 as stated in the plan) pass in `useClipDrag.test.tsx`. The correct test command is:\n```\ncd reigh-app && npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.test.tsx\n```\n\n**SD-004 \u2014 Document listener in BUBBLE phase**: The gate settled that useClipDrag must use `document.addEventListener('pointerdown', handlePointerDown)` in **bubble phase** (no `true` third arg), NOT on the wrapper element. The current code at line 744 uses capture phase (`true`). The refactored code switches to bubble phase, which works because Steps 1-3 remove all `stopPropagation` on pointerdown from ClipAction and the shot-group label. The `wrapper.contains()` containment check at line 330-331 scopes events correctly.\n\n**SD-005 \u2014 Keyboard activation on div[role=button]**: Don't invent new behavior. The current native `<button>` provides implicit Enter/Space activation that fires click. Since there's no root onClick handler on ClipAction, and selection is driven by useClipDrag pointer events, simply adding `tabIndex={0}` for focusability is sufficient. Don't add an `onKeyDown` for Enter/Space unless you can map it to `onSelect` \u2014 and even then, `onSelect` is a menu helper, not click-to-select. Keep it simple.\n\n**Overflow trigger**: The overflow trigger at ClipAction.tsx:475-499 is already a `<div role=\"button\">` with `onPointerDown stopPropagation`. The plan says: add `data-no-clip-drag` attribute to it AND remove its `onPointerDown stopPropagation`. But be careful \u2014 the overflow trigger's stopPropagation also prevents the *outer* button/div's pointerdown from firing. Since we're converting the outer element from `<button>` to `<div>`, there's no native button activation to worry about, but the event *will* bubble to document where useClipDrag's listener will see it. That's why `data-no-clip-drag` is needed.\n\n**Step 7 is the big one**: The main useEffect rewrite (lines 176-753) is ~580 lines. The refactored version should use the state machine pattern with AbortController. Key things to get right:\n1. `endSession` must be callable from any state and idempotent\n2. `pendingOpsRef.current += 1` happens in handlePointerDown (on entering pending), `pendingOpsRef.current -= 1` happens in endSession\n3. `interactionState.drag = true` and `setGestureOwner('clip')` happen on threshold cross (pending\u2192dragging), not on pointerdown\n4. `dragSessionRef.current` must be set to a backward-compatible object on entering pending (not just dragging), because TimelineCanvas checks truthiness to hide shot-group overlays during any active drag session\n5. The `deferDeactivate` pattern (rAF for crossTrackActive reset) must be preserved for cross-track drops\n\n**Ref consolidation**: Replace the 15 individual ref pairs (lines 147-174) with a single `latestRef = useRef({...})` pattern. Update on every render: `latestRef.current = { coordinator, moveClipToRow, ... }`. In handlers, read `latestRef.current.coordinator` instead of `coordinatorRef.current`.\n\n**CSS selectors**: The plan assumes styling uses `.clip-action` class selectors, not `button.clip-action`. Verify no CSS in the project uses `button.clip-action` before changing the element type.",

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace

        Idea:
Refactor the video editor timeline drag system (useClipDrag.ts and related) to be elegant and well-engineered. The current system has these problems we've identified:

1. **Two competing pointer event systems**: useClipDrag bolts a capture-phase document listener on top of the timeline library's own pointer system, coordinated via a fragile string-mutex gestureOwner ref. If either side fails to release ownership, the other is permanently locked out.

2. **Pointer capture + React re-renders = race condition**: setPointerCapture pins events to a DOM element, but React can replace that element during re-render, silently releasing capture and potentially orphaning the session.

3. **The button element fights you**: Native button focus, activation, click synthesis, and drag behavior all interfere with the custom pointer system. stopPropagation() on pointerdown creates asymmetry between capture and bubble phases.

4. **Session lifecycle is optimistic**: Every pointerdown creates a mutable DragSession with 20+ fields. Cleanup relies on pointerup/pointercancel always firing with matching pointerId. Leaked sessions leave listeners on window, pendingOps stuck > 0, and gestureOwner stuck.

5. **600-line effect closure with 15 ref-of-ref indirections**: The entire drag system lives in one useEffect that never re-runs. Every changing value is smuggled through refs (selectedClipIdsRefRef, pendingOpsRefRef, etc.). Nothing is statically traceable.

6. **DragSession is a grab-bag**: Mixes pointer tracking, DOM references, input classification, gesture state, and event handlers in one mutated object.

The well-engineered version should have:
- **Single event coordinator** instead of two competing systems
- **AbortController pattern** for atomic listener cleanup (no leaked sessions)
- **No pointer capture** — just window listeners + coordinator state machine
- **div role=button instead of button** — same a11y, no native behaviors to fight
- **Explicit state machine**: idle → pending → dragging → idle, with each transition having exactly one set of side effects
- **Derived values, not accumulated mutations**: ghost position and snap as pure functions of (dragState, pointerPosition, timelineData)
- **No pendingOps counter** — just read dragState !== idle

Key files:
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.ts (main target, ~760 lines)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/ClipAction.tsx (button → div role=button, remove stopPropagation)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useMarqueeSelect.ts (the other pointer system that competes)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx (wires everything together)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.test.tsx (tests)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/mobile-interaction-model.ts (gesture owner types)
- reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/interaction-state.ts (interaction state ref)

The goal is to make this system simple, correct, and debuggable while preserving all existing functionality (single clip drag, multi-clip drag, cross-track drag, shot group drag, marquee select, click-to-select, modifier key selection, snap-to-edge, auto-scroll during drag).

  "meta_commentary": "## Critical Execution Guidance\n\n**Test baseline**: 15 tests (not 17 as stated in the plan) pass in `useClipDrag.test.tsx`. The correct test command is:\n```\ncd reigh-app && npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.test.tsx\n```\n\n**SD-004 \u2014 Document listener in BUBBLE phase**: The gate settled that useClipDrag must use `document.addEventListener('pointerdown', handlePointerDown)` in **bubble phase** (no `true` third arg), NOT on the wrapper element. The current code at line 744 uses capture phase (`true`). The refactored code switches to bubble phase, which works because Steps 1-3 remove all `stopPropagation` on pointerdown from ClipAction and the shot-group label. The `wrapper.contains()` containment check at line 330-331 scopes events correctly.\n\n**SD-005 \u2014 Keyboard activation on div[role=button]**: Don't invent new behavior. The current native `<button>` provides implicit Enter/Space activation that fires click. Since there's no root onClick handler on ClipAction, and selection is driven by useClipDrag pointer events, simply adding `tabIndex={0}` for focusability is sufficient. Don't add an `onKeyDown` for Enter/Space unless you can map it to `onSelect` \u2014 and even then, `onSelect` is a menu helper, not click-to-select. Keep it simple.\n\n**Overflow trigger**: The overflow trigger at ClipAction.tsx:475-499 is already a `<div role=\"button\">` with `onPointerDown stopPropagation`. The plan says: add `data-no-clip-drag` attribute to it AND remove its `onPointerDown stopPropagation`. But be careful \u2014 the overflow trigger's stopPropagation also prevents the *outer* button/div's pointerdown from firing. Since we're converting the outer element from `<button>` to `<div>`, there's no native button activation to worry about, but the event *will* bubble to document where useClipDrag's listener will see it. That's why `data-no-clip-drag` is needed.\n\n**Step 7 is the big one**: The main useEffect rewrite (lines 176-753) is ~580 lines. The refactored version should use the state machine pattern with AbortController. Key things to get right:\n1. `endSession` must be callable from any state and idempotent\n2. `pendingOpsRef.current += 1` happens in handlePointerDown (on entering pending), `pendingOpsRef.current -= 1` happens in endSession\n3. `interactionState.drag = true` and `setGestureOwner('clip')` happen on threshold cross (pending\u2192dragging), not on pointerdown\n4. `dragSessionRef.current` must be set to a backward-compatible object on entering pending (not just dragging), because TimelineCanvas checks truthiness to hide shot-group overlays during any active drag session\n5. The `deferDeactivate` pattern (rAF for crossTrackActive reset) must be preserved for cross-track drops\n\n**Ref consolidation**: Replace the 15 individual ref pairs (lines 147-174) with a single `latestRef = useRef({...})` pattern. Update on every render: `latestRef.current = { coordinator, moveClipToRow, ... }`. In handlers, read `latestRef.current.coordinator` instead of `coordinatorRef.current`.\n\n**CSS selectors**: The plan assumes styling uses `.clip-action` class selectors, not `button.clip-action`. Verify no CSS in the project uses `button.clip-action` before changing the element type.",

- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies — even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

AbortController()'), 'useClipDrag is missing AbortController session cleanup');\n+assert(clipDrag.includes('controller.abort()'), 'useClipDrag does not abort session listeners');\n+assert(clipDrag.includes(\"phase: 'pending'\"), 'useClipDrag is missing the pending drag state');\n+assert(clipDrag.includes(\"phase: 'dragging'\"), 'useClipDrag is missing the dragging drag state');\n+assert(clipDrag.includes('latestRef.current = {'), 'useClipDrag is missing the consolidated latestRef pattern');\n+assert(!clipDrag.includes('selectedClipIdsRefRef'), 'useClipDrag still contains legacy ref-of-ref plumbing');\n+assert(clipAction.includes('role=\"button\"'), 'ClipAction no longer renders the drag shell with role=\"button\"');\n+\n+console.log('clip-drag structural regression check passed');\n*** End Patch\n"
}
